\displaystyle Wr+\widehat{P}^{\pi^{\star}}\widehat{h}-\left(c^{\pi^{\star}}-\alpha\mathbf{1}\right)\widehat{\xi}\widehat{h}+\widehat{\lambda}\alpha\mathbf{1}

\displaystyle=\left(r^{\pi^{\star}}+(c^{\pi^{\star}}-\alpha\mathbf{1})(r(\tilde{s},0)-r(\tilde{s},1))\right)+\widehat{P}^{\pi^{\star}}\widehat{h}-\left(c^{\pi^{\star}}-\alpha\mathbf{1}\right)\left(r(\tilde{s},0)-r(\tilde{s},1)-\widehat{\lambda}\right)+\widehat{\lambda}\alpha\mathbf{1}

\displaystyle=r^{\pi^{\star}}+\widehat{P}^{\pi^{\star}}\widehat{h}+\widehat{\lambda}c^{\pi^{\star}}

Combining this with (
47
), we obtain that

\displaystyle r^{\pi^{\star}}+\widehat{P}^{\pi^{\star}}\widehat{h}+\widehat{\lambda}c^{\pi^{\star}}=\widehat{h}+\widehat{\zeta}\mathbf{1}.

(48)

Finally we check the equality of the rows involving the neutral state. As discussed above, the definition (
29
) of
\widehat{\lambda}
is

\widehat{\lambda}=r(\tilde{s},0)-r(\tilde{s},1)-\widehat{\xi}\widehat{h}=r(\tilde{s},0)-r(\tilde{s},1)-\widehat{\xi}\widehat{h}=r(\tilde{s},0)-r(\tilde{s},1)-(\widehat{P}_{\tilde{s}1}-\widehat{P}_{\tilde{s}0})\widehat{h}

which directly rearranges to

\displaystyle r(\tilde{s},0)+\widehat{P}_{\tilde{s}0}\widehat{h}=r(\tilde{s},1)+\widehat{\lambda}+\widehat{P}_{\tilde{s}1}\widehat{h}.

(49)

Therefore, (
49
) implies

\displaystyle r^{\pi^{\star}}(\tilde{s})+e_{\tilde{s}}^{\top}\widehat{P}^{\pi^{\star}}\widehat{h}+\widehat{\lambda}c^{\pi^{\star}}(\tilde{s})

\displaystyle=\pi^{\star}(0\mid\tilde{s})\left(r(\tilde{s},0)+\widehat{P}_{\tilde{s}0}\widehat{h}\right)+\pi^{\star}(1\mid\tilde{s})\left(r(\tilde{s},1)+\widehat{\lambda}+\widehat{P}_{\tilde{s}1}\widehat{h}\right)

\displaystyle=r(\tilde{s},0)+\widehat{P}_{\tilde{s}0}\widehat{h}=r(\tilde{s},1)+\widehat{\lambda}+\widehat{P}_{\tilde{s}1}\widehat{h}.

Combining with (
48
) (specialized to just row
\tilde{s}
) we obtain

r(\tilde{s},0)+\widehat{P}_{\tilde{s}0}\widehat{h}=r(\tilde{s},1)+\widehat{\lambda}+\widehat{P}_{\tilde{s}1}\widehat{h}=\widehat{h}(\tilde{s})+\widehat{\zeta}.

Proof E.13

Proof of Lemma
8.7
.
We start by showing the first three perturbation bounds between the true dual solution and the perturbed dual candidate solution.
Using Lemma
8.5
(and that
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}
is invariant to shifts by
\mathbf{1}
), we have that

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{h}-h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}

\displaystyle=\left\|H_{U}Wr-H_{\widehat{U}}Wr\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}

\displaystyle=\left\|H_{U}(\widehat{U}-U)H_{\widehat{U}}Wr+\mathbf{1}xWr\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}

(by Lemma
E.4
)

\displaystyle=\left\|H_{U}(\widehat{U}-U)h^{\star}+H_{U}(\widehat{U}-U)(\widehat{h}-h^{\star})\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|H_{U}(\widehat{U}-U)h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\lVert\widehat{U}-U\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\|\widehat{h}-h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}.

After rearranging, we obtain the bound

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{h}-h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}\leq\frac{\left\|H_{U}(\widehat{U}-U)h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}}{1-\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\lVert\widehat{U}-U\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}}\leq\frac{\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\lVert\widehat{U}-U\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}}{1-\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\lVert\widehat{U}-U\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}}

since

\displaystyle\left\|H_{U}(\widehat{U}-U)h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}

\displaystyle=\inf_{\kappa\in\mathbb{R}}\left\|H_{U}(\widehat{U}-U)(h^{\star}-\kappa\mathbf{1})\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\inf_{\kappa\in\mathbb{R}}\left\|H_{U}(\widehat{U}-U)(h^{\star}-\kappa\mathbf{1})\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\lVert\widehat{U}-U\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\inf_{\kappa\in\mathbb{R}}\left\|h^{\star}-\kappa\mathbf{1}\right\|_{\infty}=\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\lVert\widehat{U}-U\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}.

Next, for
\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda

\displaystyle=\xi h^{\star}-\widehat{\xi}\widehat{h}=(\xi-\widehat{\xi})h^{\star}+\widehat{\xi}(h^{\star}-\widehat{h})=(\xi-\widehat{\xi})(h^{\star}-\kappa\mathbf{1})+\widehat{\xi}(h^{\star}-\widehat{h}-\kappa^{\prime}\mathbf{1})

for any
\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
, since as checked previously
\xi\mathbf{1}=\widehat{\xi}\mathbf{1}=0
. Hence

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\xi-\widehat{\xi}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\inf_{\kappa\in\mathbb{R}}\left\|h^{\star}-\kappa\mathbf{1}\right\|_{\infty}+\inf_{\kappa^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}}\left\lVert\widehat{\xi}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\|h^{\star}-\widehat{h}-\kappa^{\prime}\mathbf{1}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\lVert\xi-\widehat{\xi}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+\frac{1}{2}\left\lVert\widehat{\xi}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\|\widehat{h}-h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\lVert\xi-\widehat{\xi}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+\left\|\widehat{h}-h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}

using that
\left\lVert\widehat{\xi}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}=\left\lVert\widehat{P}_{\tilde{s}1}-\widehat{P}_{\tilde{s}0}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq\left\lVert\widehat{P}_{\tilde{s}1}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}+\left\lVert\widehat{P}_{\tilde{s}0}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}=2
.
Next we bound
|\widehat{\zeta}-\zeta|
. We have, for an arbitrary
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}
, that

\displaystyle\widehat{\zeta}-\zeta=\widehat{\mu}Wr+\widehat{\lambda}\alpha-\mu^{\star}Wr+\lambda\alpha

\displaystyle=(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)Wr+(\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda)\alpha

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mu}(\widehat{U}-U)H_{U}Wr+(\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda)\alpha=\widehat{\mu}(\widehat{U}-U)(h^{\star}+\kappa\mathbf{1})+(\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda)\alpha

using the identity for
\widehat{\mu}-\mu^{\star}
shown in Lemma
8.1
as well as that
h^{\star}
is equal to
H_{U}Wr
plus a shift by a multiple of
\mathbf{1}
(by Lemma
8.5
) and
(\widehat{U}-U)\mathbf{1}=\mathbf{0}
(by Lemma
E.2
).
Now by triangle inequality we can bound

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\zeta}-\zeta\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\widehat{\mu}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\lVert\widehat{U}-U\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\inf_{\kappa\in\mathbb{R}}\left\|h^{\star}+\kappa\mathbf{1}\right\|_{\infty}+\left|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda\right||\alpha|\leq\left\lVert\widehat{U}-U\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\frac{1}{2}\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+\left|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda\right|

since
\left\lVert\widehat{\mu}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}=1
(because Lemma
8.4
implies that it is a probability distribution) and
|\alpha|\leq 1
.

Now we show the final part of the lemma statement.
Our goal is equivalently to show that elementwise

M\left(r+\widehat{\lambda}\tilde{e}+\widehat{P}\widehat{h}-\widehat{\zeta}-J\widehat{h}\right)<\mathbf{0},

where
M
is the matrix which selects only the rows in the conclusion of the lemma, that is,
M\in\mathbb{R}^{(S-1)\times(SA)}
, each row of
M
is only supported on the entries
(s,a)
such that
s\neq\tilde{s}
and
\pi^{\star}(a\mid s)=1
, and each row of
M
is distinct and has only one nonzero entry.
We start from the identity

r+\widehat{\lambda}\tilde{e}+\widehat{P}\widehat{h}-\widehat{\zeta}-J\widehat{h}=(\widehat{P}-P)h^{\star}+(\widehat{P}-J)(\widehat{h}-h^{\star})+(\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda)\tilde{e}-(\widehat{\zeta}-\zeta)\mathbf{1}+\left(r+\lambda\tilde{e}+Ph^{\star}-\zeta-Jh^{\star}\right),

and now we will apply the projection matrix
M
and attempt to upper-bound the result. We calculate

M\left(r+\widehat{\lambda}\tilde{e}+\widehat{P}\widehat{h}-\widehat{\zeta}-J\widehat{h}\right)\\
\begin{aligned} &=M\left((\widehat{P}-P)h^{\star}+(\widehat{P}-J)(\widehat{h}-h^{\star})+(\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda)\tilde{e}-(\widehat{\zeta}-\zeta)\mathbf{1}+\left(r+\lambda\tilde{e}+Ph^{\star}-\zeta-Jh^{\star}\right)\right)\\
&=M\Big((\widehat{P}-P)(h^{\star}-\kappa\mathbf{1})+(\widehat{P}-J)(\widehat{h}-h^{\star}-\kappa^{\prime}\mathbf{1})+(\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda)\tilde{e}-(\widehat{\zeta}-\zeta)\mathbf{1}+\left(r+\lambda\tilde{e}+Ph^{\star}-\zeta-Jh^{\star}\right)\Big)\\
&\leq M\left(r+\lambda\tilde{e}+Ph^{\star}-\zeta-Jh^{\star}\right)+\left\lVert\widehat{P}-P\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\|h^{\star}-\kappa\mathbf{1}\right\|_{\infty}\mathbf{1}\\
&\qquad+\left\lVert\widehat{P}-J\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\|\widehat{h}-h^{\star}-\kappa^{\prime}\mathbf{1}\right\|_{\infty}\mathbf{1}+\left|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda\right|\left\|\tilde{e}\right\|_{\infty}\mathbf{1}+\left|\widehat{\zeta}-\zeta\right|\mathbf{1}\\
&\leq M\left(r+\lambda\tilde{e}+Ph^{\star}-\zeta-Jh^{\star}\right)+\left\lVert\widehat{P}-P\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\frac{1}{2}\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}\mathbf{1}+\left\|\widehat{h}-h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}\mathbf{1}+\left|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda\right|\mathbf{1}+\left|\widehat{\zeta}-\zeta\right|\mathbf{1}\end{aligned}

using that
(\widehat{P}-P)\mathbf{1}=\mathbf{0}
and
(\widehat{P}-J)\mathbf{1}=\mathbf{0}
for the second equality, and then that
\left\lVert J\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}=\max_{sa}\left\|e_{s}\right\|_{1}=1
,
\left\|\tilde{e}\right\|_{\infty}=1
,
\left\lVert M\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}=1
, and choosing
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}
so that
\left\|h^{\star}-\kappa\mathbf{1}\right\|_{\infty}=\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}/2
for the inequality steps.

Using the first three bounds shown in this lemma, we have that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\widehat{P}-P\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\frac{1}{2}\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+\left\|\widehat{h}-h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+\left|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda\right|+\left|\widehat{\zeta}-\zeta\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta}{2}\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+\left\|\widehat{h}-h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+\left|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda\right|+\left\lVert\widehat{U}-U\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\frac{1}{2}\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+\left|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta}{2}\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+\left\|\widehat{h}-h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+2\left|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda\right|+\frac{3\delta}{2}\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\delta\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+\left\|\widehat{h}-h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+2\left(\frac{1}{2}\left\lVert\xi-\widehat{\xi}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+\left\|\widehat{h}-h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 4\delta\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+3\left\|\widehat{h}-h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}

\displaystyle\leq 4\delta\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+3\frac{\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\lVert\widehat{U}-U\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}}{1-\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\lVert\widehat{U}-U\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq 4\delta\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+3\frac{\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}3\delta\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}}{1-\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\lVert\widehat{U}-U\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}}

(50)

where also we simplified by using that
\left\lVert\widehat{P}-P\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq\delta
by assumption, that
\left\lVert\widehat{\xi}-\xi\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq 2\left\lVert\widehat{P}-P\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq 2\delta
, and

\displaystyle\left\lVert\widehat{U}-U\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert P^{\pi^{\star}}-(c^{\pi^{\star}}-\alpha\mathbf{1})\xi-\widehat{P}^{\pi^{\star}}+(c^{\pi^{\star}}-\alpha\mathbf{1})\widehat{\xi}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert P^{\pi^{\star}}-\widehat{P}^{\pi^{\star}}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}+\left\|c^{\pi^{\star}}-\alpha\mathbf{1}\right\|_{\infty}\left\lVert\xi-\widehat{\xi}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq\delta+1\cdot 2\delta=3\delta.

Note that (
25
) implies that

\left\lVert\widehat{U}-U\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2}\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{\min}^{\star}}}{\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}},

where the second inequality is because
\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu^{\star}_{\min}}}\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}
. Hence we can further simplify (
50
) and bound

\displaystyle 4\delta\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+3\frac{\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}3\delta\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}}{1-\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\lVert\widehat{U}-U\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq 4\delta\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+3\frac{\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}3\delta\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}}{1/2}

\displaystyle=4\delta\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+18\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\delta\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}.

Finally, letting

G=\min_{sa:y^{\star}(s,a)=0}\zeta^{\star}(s)+h^{\star}(s)-r(s,a)-\lambda^{\star}\mathbb{I}(a=1)-P_{sa}h^{\star}>0,

we can combine all of our previous calculations to obtain that

\displaystyle M\left(r+\widehat{\lambda}\tilde{e}+\widehat{P}\widehat{h}-\widehat{\zeta}-J\widehat{h}\right)

\displaystyle\leq M\left(r+\lambda\tilde{e}+Ph^{\star}-\zeta-Jh^{\star}\right)+(4\delta\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+18\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\delta\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}})\mathbf{1}

\displaystyle\leq-G\mathbf{1}+(4\delta\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+18\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\delta\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}})\mathbf{1}

which is elementwise strictly less than
\mathbf{0}
when

\delta\leq\frac{G/2}{4\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+18\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}},

as desired.

E.6
Proof of Lemma
8.8

Proof E.14

Proof of Lemma
8.8
.
Now we show uniqueness of the primal and dual optimal solutions. First, letting
\tilde{y}
be another optimal solution to the perturbed primal LP, then
\tilde{y}
and
(\widehat{\zeta},\widehat{\lambda},\widehat{h})
must satisfy the complementary slackness property (
13
) (as optimal solutions). Since we have checked in Lemma
8.7
that the dual constraints are strict for all
s,a
such that
y^{\star}(s,a)=0
, this implies that we must have that
\tilde{y}(s,a)=0
for all
s,a
such that
y^{\star}(s,a)=0
. This implies that, letting
\tilde{\mu}(s)=\tilde{y}(s,0)+\tilde{y}(s,1)
, we have that
\tilde{y}=\tilde{\mu}W
, since
\tilde{y}
is supported on a (possibly non-proper) subset of the support of
y^{\star}
, and also
\tilde{y}\tilde{e}=\alpha
by its feasibility for the primal perturbed LP. Then since primal feasibility also guarantees
\tilde{y}P=\tilde{y}J
, we have

\tilde{\mu}\widehat{U}=\tilde{\mu}W\widehat{P}=\tilde{y}\widehat{P}=\tilde{y}J=\tilde{\mu}WJ=\tilde{\mu}I=\tilde{\mu}.

From the fact that
\tilde{\mu}\widehat{U}=\tilde{\mu}
, we can derive that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mu}(\widehat{U}-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu})=\tilde{\mu}\widehat{U}-\tilde{\mu}\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}=\tilde{\mu}-\widehat{\mu}\quad\implies\quad

\displaystyle\widehat{\mu}=\tilde{\mu}\left(I-(\widehat{U}-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\implies\quad

\displaystyle\tilde{\mu}=\widehat{\mu}\left(I-(\widehat{U}-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu})\right)^{-1}=\widehat{\mu}H_{\widehat{U}}+\widehat{\mu}\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}=\widehat{\mu}

using that
\tilde{\mu}\mathbf{1}=\tilde{\mu}W\mathbf{1}=\tilde{y}\mathbf{1}=1
by primal feasibility of
\tilde{y}
in the first line, and then invertibility of
I-(\widehat{U}-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu})
(by Lemma
8.3
) and then the facts that
\widehat{\mu}H_{\widehat{U}}=\mathbf{0}
and
\widehat{\mu}\mathbf{1}=1
(both from Lemma
E.4
) in the final line. Finally,
\widehat{\mu}=\tilde{\mu}
implies that
\tilde{y}=\tilde{\mu}W=\widehat{\mu}W=\widehat{y}
, so we have shown uniqueness of
\widehat{y}
.

Now we show the uniqueness properties of the dual solution.
These steps are analogous to those of Lemma
8.5
. Let
(\tilde{\zeta},\tilde{\lambda},\tilde{h})
be some dual optimal solution.
By complementary slackness between
(\tilde{\zeta},\tilde{\lambda},\tilde{h})
and
\widehat{y}
, for all
s,a
such that
\widehat{y}(s,a)>0
we have
r(s,a)+\tilde{\lambda}\mathbb{I}(a=1)+\widehat{P}_{sa}\tilde{h}=\tilde{\zeta}+\tilde{h}(s)
. By the construction of
\widehat{y}
and Lemma
8.4
, these are exactly the
s,a
such that
y^{\star}(s,a)>0
. Each row of
W
is only supported on such such
(s,a)
, so we have that

W(r+\tilde{\lambda}+\widehat{P}\tilde{h})=W(\tilde{\zeta}\mathbf{1}+J\tilde{h}).

Using the properties of
W
from Lemma
E.6
that
W\widehat{P}=\widehat{U}
,
W\mathbf{1}=\mathbf{1}
,
WJ=I
, and
W\tilde{e}=\alpha\mathbf{1}
, the above simplifies to

Wr+\tilde{\lambda}\alpha\mathbf{1}+\widehat{U}\tilde{h}=\tilde{\zeta}\mathbf{1}+\tilde{h}.

Since
(\tilde{\zeta},\tilde{\lambda},\tilde{h})
is dual optimal and has the same objective value as
\widehat{y}
for the perturbed primal LP, we have that
\tilde{\zeta}=\widehat{\mu}Wr+\tilde{\lambda}\alpha
. Combining this with the previous display equation, we obtain that

(I-\widehat{U})\tilde{h}=Wr-\widehat{\mu}Wr\mathbf{1}.

After rewriting this as
(I-(\widehat{U}-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}))\tilde{h}=(I-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu})Wr+\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}\tilde{h}
and then multiplying by
(I-(\widehat{U}-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}))^{-1}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\tilde{h}

\displaystyle=(I-(\widehat{U}-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}))^{-1}\left((I-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu})Wr+\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}\tilde{h}\right)

\displaystyle=(I-(\widehat{U}-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}))^{-1}Wr-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}Wr+\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}\tilde{h}=H_{\widehat{U}}Wr+\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}\tilde{h}=\widehat{h}+\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}\tilde{h}

using that
(I-(\widehat{U}-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}))^{-1}\mathbf{1}=\mathbf{1}
, which follows from
H_{\widehat{U}}\mathbf{1}=\mathbf{0}
(Lemma
E.4
).
The equalities implied by the aforementioned complementary slackness between between
(\tilde{\zeta},\tilde{\lambda},\tilde{h})
and
\widehat{y}
also ensure that

r(\tilde{s},0)+\widehat{P}_{\tilde{s}0}\tilde{h}=\tilde{\zeta}+\tilde{h}(\tilde{s})=r(\tilde{s},1)+\tilde{\lambda}+\widehat{P}_{\tilde{s}1}\tilde{h}

(by considering
(s,a)=(\tilde{s},0)
and
(\tilde{s},1)
). Hence by rearranging we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\lambda}=r(\tilde{s},0)-r(\tilde{s},1)-\widehat{P}_{\tilde{s}1}\tilde{h}+\widehat{P}_{\tilde{s}0}\tilde{h}

\displaystyle=r(\tilde{s},0)-r(\tilde{s},1)-\widehat{\xi}\tilde{h}

\displaystyle=r(\tilde{s},0)-r(\tilde{s},1)-\widehat{\xi}(\widehat{h}+\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}\tilde{h})=r(\tilde{s},0)-r(\tilde{s},1)-\widehat{\xi}\widehat{h}=\widehat{\lambda}

using the fact that
\widehat{\xi}\mathbf{1}=\mathbf{0}
and the definition (
29
) of
\widehat{\lambda}
.
Finally, we have already shown that
\tilde{\zeta}=\widehat{\mu}Wr+\tilde{\lambda}\alpha
, but now since we have also shown that
\tilde{\lambda}=\widehat{\lambda}
, by combining with the definition (
30
) of
\widehat{\zeta}
, we furthermore can conclude that
\tilde{\zeta}=\widehat{\zeta}
.

\square

E.7
Proof of Lemma
8.9

As shown in the proof of Lemma
8.7
, we have
\left\lVert\widehat{\xi}-\xi\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq 2\delta
and
\left\lVert\widehat{U}-U\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq 3\delta
. We will now use these to simplify the various perturbation bounds.
From Lemma
8.4
, we have

\left\|\widehat{y}-y^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\left\lVert\widehat{U}-U\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq 3\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\delta.

As shown in the proof of Lemma
8.7
, under the condition (
25
) we have

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{h}-h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\lVert\widehat{U}-U\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}}{1-\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\lVert\widehat{U}-U\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}}\leq 2\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\lVert\widehat{U}-U\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}.

Hence

\left\|\widehat{h}-h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}\leq 6\delta\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}.

Now for the other perturbation bounds from Lemma
8.7
, we can simplify them as

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\lVert\xi-\widehat{\xi}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+\left\|\widehat{h}-h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}\leq\delta\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+6\delta\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}

and

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\zeta}-\zeta\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\widehat{U}-U\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\frac{1}{2}\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+\left|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda\right|\leq\frac{3}{2}\delta\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+\delta\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}+6\delta\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\|h^{\star}\right\|_{\textnormal{span}}.

Finally, we confirm that all assumptions are satisfied for all the lemmas used in this proof. To ensure (
25
), if we require

\delta\leq\frac{\sqrt{\mu^{\star}_{\min}}}{6}\frac{1-\left\lVert\Phi\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}}{1+{\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}}/{\sqrt{\mu^{\star}_{\min}}}}

then we will have

\left\lVert\widehat{U}-U\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}\leq 3\delta\leq\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-\left\lVert U-\mathbf{1}\mu^{\star}\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}}{1+{\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}}/{\sqrt{\mu^{\star}_{\min}}}}

as needed. To ensure (
27
), if we require

\delta\leq\min_{sa:y^{\star}(s,a)>0}\frac{y^{\star}(s,a)}{6\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}}

then we will have

\left\lVert\widehat{U}-U\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}\leq 3\delta\leq\min_{sa:y^{\star}(s,a)>0}\frac{y^{\star}(s,a)}{2\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}}

as needed.

E.8
Proof of Lemma
8.10

By support preservation,
\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}(s)=\bar{\pi}^{\star}(s)
for all

s\neq\tilde{s}
. Hence the two policies can differ only at the neutral
state
\tilde{s}
. By the perturbation bound for
\widehat{y}^{\star}
, and by the definition of

\delta_{\min}
, for each
a\in\{0,1\}
,

|y^{\star}(\tilde{s},a)-\widehat{y}^{\star}(\tilde{s},a)|\leq 3\delta\left\lVert H_{U}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq\frac{\mu^{\star}_{\min}}{48S\tau(5+\log_{2}S)}.

Since

\bar{\pi}^{\star}(a\mid\tilde{s})=\frac{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},a)}{\mu^{\star}(\tilde{s})},\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}(a\mid\tilde{s})=\frac{\widehat{y}^{\star}(\tilde{s},a)}{\widehat{\mu}^{\star}(\tilde{s})},

and

|\widehat{\mu}^{\star}(\tilde{s})-\mu^{\star}(\tilde{s})|\leq\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}|y^{\star}(\tilde{s},a)-\widehat{y}^{\star}(\tilde{s},a)|,

we obtain

|\bar{\pi}^{\star}(a\mid\tilde{s})-\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}(a\mid\tilde{s})|\leq\frac{1}{16S\tau(5+\log_{2}S)},\qquad a\in\{0,1\}.

Therefore,

\left\lVert P^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}}-P^{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}|\bar{\pi}^{\star}(a\mid\tilde{s})-\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}(a\mid\tilde{s})|\leq\frac{1}{8S\tau(5+\log_{2}S)}.

On the other hand, by convexity of the
\ell_{1}
norm and the fifth term in the
definition of
\delta_{\min}
,

\left\lVert P^{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}}-\widehat{P}^{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq\max_{s,a}\|P(\cdot\mid s,a)-\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid s,a)\|_{1}\leq\delta\leq\frac{1}{8S\tau(5+\log_{2}S)}.

Combining the last two displays gives

\left\lVert P^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}}-\widehat{P}^{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq\frac{1}{4S\tau(5+\log_{2}S)}.

Lemma
B.2
, applied with

\mathcal{Z}=\mathcal{S}
,
Q=P^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}}
, and

\widetilde{Q}=\widehat{P}^{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}}
, implies that the Markov chain induced by

\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}
under
\widehat{P}
is aperiodic and unichain, with mixing time at
most
(3+\log_{2}S)\tau
.

It remains to identify its stationary distribution. By feasibility of the
perturbed primal solution,

\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\widehat{\mu}^{\star}(s)\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}(1\mid s)=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\widehat{y}^{\star}(s,1)=\alpha.

Recall that

\widehat{U}=\widehat{P}^{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}}-(c^{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}}-\alpha\mathbf{1})\widehat{\xi}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mu}^{\star}=\widehat{\mu}^{\star}\widehat{U}=\widehat{\mu}^{\star}\widehat{P}^{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}}-\widehat{\mu}^{\star}(c^{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}}-\alpha\mathbf{1})\widehat{\xi}=\widehat{\mu}^{\star}\widehat{P}^{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}}-\left(\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\widehat{\mu}^{\star}(s)\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}(1\mid s)-\alpha\right)\widehat{\xi}=\widehat{\mu}^{\star}\widehat{P}^{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}}.

So
\widehat{\mu}^{\star}
is stationary for
\widehat{P}^{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}}
. Part 1 shows

\widehat{\mu}^{\star}(s)>0
for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
. Since the chain has a single
recurrent class and its stationary distribution puts positive mass on
every state, no state can be transient. Hence the chain is irreducible. This proves part 6
and completes the proof of
Theorem
4.3
.

Appendix F
Preliminaries for the proof of Theorem
4.2

F.1
Two-set Policy

Two dynamic sets.

At each time
t
, the two-set policy maintains disjoint subsets:
(i) Set
D_{t}^{\mathrm{OL}}
on which we run
Optimal Local Control
to harvest near-optimal
reward; (ii) Set
D_{t}^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}}
on which we run
Unconstrained Optimal Control

to steer arms toward
\mu^{\star}
and eventually merge them into
D_{t}^{\mathrm{OL}}
.
The remaining arms act as a buffer to satisfy the global constraint
\sum_{i}A_{t}(i)=\alpha N
exactly.

Input:

LP solution
y^{\star}
, induced policy
\bar{\pi}^{\star}
, feasibility parameters
(\eta,\epsilon_{N}^{\mathrm{fe}})
, and tolerance
\epsilon_{N}^{\mathrm{rd}}

.

Output:

actions
(A_{t}(i))_{i\in[N]}
at each time
t

.

1

Initialize
D_{-1}^{\mathrm{OL}}\leftarrow\emptyset
and

D_{-1}^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}}\leftarrow\emptyset
.
Set
\omega\leftarrow\min\{\alpha,1-\alpha\}
.

2

for

t=0,1,2,\ldots

do

3

Select
D_{t}^{\mathrm{OL}}
: if
\delta(\bm{X}_{t},[N])\geq 0
, set

D_{t}^{\mathrm{OL}}\leftarrow[N]
; otherwise choose an

\epsilon_{N}^{\mathrm{rd}}
-maximal feasible set, preserving

D_{t-1}^{\mathrm{OL}}
when it remains feasible.

4

Choose
D_{t}^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}}
such that

D_{t-1}^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}}\setminus D_{t}^{\mathrm{OL}}\subseteq D_{t}^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}}\subseteq[N]\setminus D_{t}^{\mathrm{OL}},|D_{t}^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}}|=\left\lfloor\omega\bigl(N-|D_{t}^{\mathrm{OL}}|\bigr)\right\rfloor.

5

Apply Optimal Local Control on
D_{t}^{\mathrm{OL}}
, Unconstrained
Optimal Control on
D_{t}^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}}
, and use the remaining arms
as a buffer to enforce
\sum_{i}A_{t}(i)=\alpha N
.

Algorithm 3

Two-set policy

Subroutines.

For a subset
D
with state counts
z(s)=|\{i\in D:S_{t}(i)=s\}|
:
(i)
Unconstrained Optimal Control
activates an expected
\bar{\pi}^{\star}(1\mid s)\,z(s)
arms in each state
s

(using randomized rounding to handle integrality);
(ii)
Optimal Local Control
draws a target budget
B\in\{\lfloor\alpha|D|\rfloor,\lceil\alpha|D|\rceil\}
with

\mathbb{E}[B]=\alpha|D|
, and then allocates actions in
D
following a priority rule and using the neutral state
\tilde{s}
to meet the budget constraint.

Augmented state space.

Note that the Two-set policy induces a finite-state Markov chain whose state at time
t
is
(\bm{X}_{t},D_{t}^{\mathrm{OL}},D_{t}^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}})
. For a short notation, we write
\Sigma_{t}=(\bm{X}_{t},D_{t}^{\mathrm{OL}},D_{t}^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}})
.

F.2
\mu^{\star}
-weighted norms and operator norms

With Assumption
4.2
, the Markov chain induced by
\bar{\pi}^{\star}
converges to a unique stationary distribution, denoted by
\mu^{\star}=(\mu^{\star}(s))_{s\in\mathbb{S}}
, and this Markov chain has no transient state, which means
\mu^{\star}_{\min}\triangleq\min_{s}\mu^{\star}(s)>0
. Let
\tau
be its mixing time. From the definition of
\bar{\pi}^{\star}
, it is straightforward to verify that
\mu^{\star}(s)=y^{\star}(s,0)+y^{\star}(s,1)
. Consequently, the long-run average reward of
\bar{\pi}^{\star}
equals
\rho^{\mathrm{rel}}
, and the long-run average budget usage under
\bar{\pi}^{\star}
equals
\alpha
. Define
D_{\mu^{\star}}\triangleq\mathrm{diag}(\mu^{\star})
.
For any vector
f\in\mathbb{R}^{S}
we define the weighted inner product
\langle f,g\rangle_{\mu^{\star}}\triangleq\sum_{s\in S}\mu^{\star}(s)\,f(s)g(s)
and weighted norm
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}}\triangleq\big(\langle f,f\rangle_{\mu^{\star}}\big)^{1/2}=\big\|D_{\mu^{\star}}^{1/2}f\big\|_{2}.

They correspond to the Euclidean space
L_{2}(\mu^{\star})
equipped with the RMS weight
\mu^{\star}
.
We also use the induced norm

\left\lVert A\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}\triangleq\sup_{f\neq 0}\frac{\left\lVert Af\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}}}{\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}}}=\big\|D_{\mu^{\star}}^{1/2}\,A\,D_{\mu^{\star}}^{-1/2}\big\|_{2}.

The last equality shows that the
L_{2}(\mu^{\star})
–operator norm is the spectral norm of
the similarity transform
D_{\mu^{\star}}^{1/2}AD_{\mu^{\star}}^{-1/2}
.
For comparison, we recall the induced
\ell_{\infty}
operator norm for a matrix
A\in\mathbb{R}^{S\times S}
:

\left\lVert A\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\triangleq\sup_{f\neq 0}\frac{\|Af\|_{\infty}}{\|f\|_{\infty}}=\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}|A(s,s^{\prime})|,

which coincides with the maximum absolute row sum of
A
.
It is easy to check that

(\mu^{\star}_{\min})^{1/2}\;\left\lVert A\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq\left\lVert A\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}\leq(\mu^{\star}_{\min})^{-1/2}\;\left\lVert A\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}.

These inequalities follow from the vector–norm equivalences

(\mu^{\star}_{\min})^{1/2}\|f\|_{\infty}\leq\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}}\leq\|f\|_{\infty}
.

F.3
Weighted
\ell_{2}
norms for quantifying distributional convergence

A recurring theme in our analysis is to control how fast the empirical state distribution of a collection of arms approaches a target stationary distribution. To this end, we follow the weighted-
\ell_{2}
approach and introduce two matrices,
W
and
U
,
which is used in
Hong et al. 2024a
and induce two weighted norms on
\mathbb{R}^{S}
.
Recall that
\mu^{\star}
is the stationary distribution of the Markov chain induced by
\bar{\pi}^{\star}
under
P
, and the matrix
\Phi
is defined in (
6
).

Definition F.1

Define
W,U\in\mathbb{R}^{S\times S}
by

W\;\triangleq\;\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(P^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}}-\mathbf{1}\mu^{\star})^{k}\bigl((P^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}}-\mathbf{1}\mu^{\star})^{\top}\bigr)^{k}\text{ and }U\;\triangleq\;\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Phi^{k}(\Phi^{\top})^{k}.

The matrices
W
and
U
satisfy the identities

W\;=\;I+(P^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}}-\mathbf{1}\mu^{\star})W(P^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}}-\mathbf{1}\mu^{\star})^{\top}

and

U\;=\;I+\Phi\,U\,\Phi^{\top}.

They induce the weighted
\ell_{2}
norms

\|v\|_{W}\;\triangleq\;\sqrt{v^{\top}Wv}

and

\|v\|_{U}\;\triangleq\;\sqrt{v^{\top}Uv}

for
v\in\mathbb{R}^{S}.

The local stability condition in Assumption
4.2
implies that all eigenvalues of
\Phi
are less than 1. Hence Lemma 4 from
Hong et al. 2024a
guarantees that
W
and
U
are symmetric positive definite and the eigenvalues of
W,U
are in the range
[1,\lambda_{W}]
and
[1,\lambda_{U}]
, respectively, where
\lambda_{W}
and
\lambda_{U}
are the largest eigenvalues of
W
and
U
.
In particular, for all
v\in\mathbb{R}^{S}
, it holds that

\|v\|_{2}\;\leq\;\|v\|_{W}\;\leq\;\sqrt{\lambda_{W}}\,\|v\|_{2}

and

\|v\|_{2}\;\leq\;\|v\|_{U}\;\leq\;\sqrt{\lambda_{U}}\,\|v\|_{2},

which will be repeatedly used to translate between Euclidean and weighted metrics.

F.4
Subset Lyapunov functions

We used the following two subset Lyapunov functions which are introduced in
Hong et al. 2024a
as building blocks in our Lyapunov analysis. These two classes of functions quantify the convergence of state distributions for subsets of arms that follow the Unconstrained Optimal Control or the Optimal Local Control. For any system state
\bm{x}
and subset
D\subseteq[N]
, let

z(s)
be the number of arms in
D
currently in state
s\in\mathcal{S}
.
Define the normalized state-count vector
\bm{x}(D)\in\mathbb{R}^{S}
by

\bm{x}(D,s)=\frac{z(s)}{N}
, and
m(D)=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\bm{x}(D,s)=\frac{|D|}{N}
. Then define

\displaystyle h_{W}(\bm{x},D)=\|\bm{x}(D)-m(D)\mu^{\star}\|_{W},\qquad h_{U}(\bm{x},D)=\|\bm{x}(D)-m(D)\mu^{\star}\|_{U}.

(51)

F.5
Feasibility-ensuring pair

Here we briefly introduce the feasibility-ensuring pair showed in
Hong et al. 2024a
. Define the set of transient states as

\mathcal{S}^{\emptyset}:=\{s\in\mathcal{S}:\;y^{\star}(s,0)=y^{\star}(s,1)=0\}.

A sufficient condition ensuring that the
Optimal Local Control
routine can always realize
exactly

B
activations is

\sum_{s\neq\tilde{s}}\bar{\pi}^{\star}(1\mid s)\,z(s)\leq\alpha n-|\mathcal{S}^{\emptyset}|-1,\qquad\sum_{s\neq\tilde{s}}\bar{\pi}^{\star}(0\mid s)\,z(s)\leq(1-\alpha)n-|\mathcal{S}^{\emptyset}|-1.

(52)

Intuitively, (
52
) guarantees that after rounding decisions for states

s\neq\tilde{s}
, the remaining budget is nonnegative and does not exceed the available number of arms in the neutral state, so
\tilde{s}
can “close the budget”.

For parameters
(\eta,\epsilon_{N}^{\mathrm{fe}})
, define the feasibility slack

\delta(\bm{x},D):=\eta\,m(D)-\|\bm{x}(D)-m(D)\mu^{\star}\|_{U}-\epsilon_{N}^{\mathrm{fe}}.

Definition F.2

(Feasibility-ensuring pair)

A pair
(\eta,\epsilon_{N}^{\mathrm{fe}})
is called
feasibility-ensuring
if

\delta(\bm{x},D)\geq 0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\text{$D$ satisfies the integer feasibility condition \eqref{eq:integer-feasibility}.}

Equivalently, whenever the subset-level state composition
\bm{x}(D)
is sufficiently close (in
\|\cdot\|_{U}
)
to the target profile
m(D)\mu^{\star}
with margin
\eta m(D)-\epsilon_{N}^{\mathrm{fe}}
, Subroutine 2 is guaranteed
to be feasible on
D
.

In our case,
\mathcal{S}^{\emptyset}=\emptyset
. We use the following convenient explicit choice

\eta=S^{-1/2}\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),\,y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\},\qquad\epsilon_{N}^{\mathrm{fe}}=S^{-1/2}\frac{|\mathcal{S}^{\emptyset}|+1}{N}=S^{-1/2}\frac{1}{N}.

(53)

Lemma 2 in
Hong et al. 2024a
shows that this choice of
(\eta,\epsilon_{N}^{\mathrm{fe}})
is a
feasibility-ensuring pair
.

F.6
Maximal Feasible Set

In our algorithm, at each time step we select a subset of arms on which a local control routine is executed. Rather than requiring an exact maximal feasible set, we work with an approximate notion of maximality, parameterized by a small
tolerance
\epsilon_{N}^{\mathrm{rd}}\geq 0
. This tolerance allows mild slack in the maximality requirement while still ensuring that the chosen set is nearly as large as any sufficiently feasible superset.

Definition F.3

(
\epsilon_{N}^{\mathrm{rd}}
-maximal feasible set)

Fix a system state
\bm{x}
and a tolerance parameter
\epsilon_{N}^{\mathrm{rd}}\geq 0
.
A set
D\subseteq[N]
is called
\epsilon_{N}^{\mathrm{rd}}
-maximal feasible at state
\bm{x}
if the following hold:

1. Feasibility:

\delta(\bm{x},D)\geq 0
, or
D=\emptyset
;

2. Approximate maximality:
for any
D^{\prime}
such that
D\subseteq D^{\prime}\subseteq[N]
and

\delta(\bm{x},D^{\prime})\geq\epsilon_{N}^{\mathrm{rd}}
, we have

m(D^{\prime})\leq m(D)+\epsilon_{N}^{\mathrm{rd}}.

When
\epsilon_{N}^{\mathrm{rd}}=0
, Definition
F.3
reduces to the usual notion of a
maximal element (under set inclusion) in the family

\{D^{\prime}\subseteq[N]:\delta(\bm{x},D^{\prime})\geq 0\}\cup\{\emptyset\}
.
In our theoretical development we typically take
\epsilon_{N}^{\mathrm{rd}}=\Theta(1/N)
, matching the
granularity of the mass
m(D)
, since
m(D)
changes in increments of
1/N
when adding or removing a
single arm.

F.7
Lyapunov functions

In this subsection we introduce the Lyapunov function we use. We constructed our Lyapunov function based on the Lyapunov function
\tilde{V}
introduced in
Hong et al. 2024a
. For any system state
\sigma=(\bm{x},d^{\mathrm{OL}},d^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}})
,

\tilde{V}(\sigma)=\tilde{V}(\bm{x},d^{\mathrm{OL}},d^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}})=h_{U}(\bm{x},d^{\mathrm{OL}})+h_{W}(\bm{x},d^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}})+L(1-m(d^{\mathrm{OL}})),

(54)

where
h_{U},h_{W}
are defined in (
51
),
L=2\lambda_{U}^{1/2}+2\lambda_{W}^{1/2}-2\lambda_{W}^{1/2}\min(\alpha,1-\alpha)
.
It is shown in
Hong et al. 2024a
that

\forall\sigma,0\leq\tilde{V}(\sigma)\leq\tilde{V}_{\max}\triangleq\lambda_{U}^{1/2}+\lambda_{W}^{1/2}+L

(55)

Define our Lyapunov function
V
as

V(\sigma)=V(\bm{x},d^{\mathrm{OL}},d^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}})=(\tilde{V}-\frac{\bar{\eta}}{2})^{+}+\frac{\gamma}{4(\beta+r_{\max})}(\mu^{\star}-\bm{x}([N]))Qb,

(56)

where

\begin{gathered}\beta=\sup_{\sigma}\left|(\mu^{\star}-\bm{x}([N]))Qb-\mathbb{E}\left[(\mu^{\star}-\bm{X}_{t}([N]))Qb\mid\Sigma_{t}=\sigma\right]\right|,\quad Q=(I-\Phi)^{-1},\\
b=\left(\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}(r(s,a)-r(\tilde{s},a))\bar{\pi}^{\star}(a\mid s)\right)_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\in\mathbb{R}^{S},\quad\bar{\eta}=\eta-\epsilon_{N}^{\mathrm{fe}},\\
\gamma=\frac{\bar{\eta}}{K_{Vh}},K_{Vh}=2\max\{\lambda_{W},\lambda_{U}\}\left(1+\frac{L\lambda_{U}^{1/2}}{\eta\omega}\right),\quad\omega=\min\{\alpha,1-\alpha\}.\end{gathered}

(57)

\Phi
is defined in (
6
) and
\eta,\epsilon_{N}^{\mathrm{fe}}
are defined in (
53
). It is obvious that
\beta
is a finite number since it is the maximum over a finite set. And notice that in order to make sure
\bar{\eta}>0
, we need

N>\frac{1}{\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}}.

Appendix G
Proof details of the lemmas used in Theorem
4.2

G.1
Proof of Lemma
7.2

Proof G.1

Proof
We use the Lyapunov function
V
defined in (
56
) (see Appendix
F.7
).
The following two lemmas provide the backbone of the Lyapunov analysis, which are corresponding to
(C1)
and
(C2)
in the Lyapunov framework respectively.

Lemma G.2

When
N\geq\left(\frac{4M}{\gamma}\right)^{2}
, the Lyapunov function
V
defined in (
56
) satisfies

\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}[V(\Sigma_{t+1})\mid\Sigma_{t}=\sigma]-V(\sigma)\leq-\frac{\gamma}{4(\beta+r_{\max})}g(\sigma)+6\exp(8)S\tilde{V}_{\max}\exp\left(-CN\right),

where
\gamma,\beta
are defined in (
57
),
\tilde{V}_{\max}
is defined in (
55
), and

g(\sigma)=r_{\max}\mathbb{I}[d^{\mathrm{OL}}\neq[N]]+(\mu^{\star}-\bm{x}([N]))b\cdot\mathbb{I}[d^{\mathrm{OL}}=[N]],

(58)

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle=\frac{L}{2K_{Vh}\omega}\left(1+\frac{2S^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{\eta}\right)+5\lambda_{U}^{1/2}S+3\lambda_{W}^{1/2}S+(\lambda_{W}^{1/2}+\lambda_{U}^{1/2})\exp\left(\frac{1}{2\lambda_{U}^{3}S^{3}}+32\right)\frac{16\lambda_{U}^{3/2}S^{2}}{\eta}+2\lambda_{W}^{1/2},

(59)

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle=\frac{\min\{\gamma^{2}/4,1\}}{4\max\{\lambda_{W},\lambda_{U}\}S^{2}}.

Proof G.3

Proof
We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}[V(\Sigma_{t+1})\mid\Sigma_{t}=\sigma]-V(\sigma)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}\left[\left(\tilde{V}(\Sigma_{t+1})-\frac{\bar{\eta}}{2}\right)^{+}\mid\Sigma_{t}=\sigma\right]-\left(\tilde{V}(\sigma)-\frac{\bar{\eta}}{2}\right)^{+}

\displaystyle+\frac{\gamma}{4(\beta+r_{\max})}\left(\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}\left[(\mu^{\star}-\bm{X}_{t}([N]))Qb\mid\Sigma_{t}=\sigma\right]-(\mu^{\star}-\bm{x}([N]))Qb\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{4}\mathbb{I}[d^{\mathrm{OL}}\neq[N]]+6\exp(8)S\tilde{V}_{\max}\exp\left(-CN\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\gamma}{4(\beta+r_{\max})}\left(\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}\left[(\mu^{\star}-\bm{X}_{t}([N]))Qb\mid\Sigma_{t}=\sigma\right]-(\mu^{\star}-\bm{x}([N]))Qb\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{4}\mathbb{I}[d^{\mathrm{OL}}\neq[N]]+6\exp(8)S\tilde{V}_{\max}\exp\left(-CN\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{4(\beta+r_{\max})}\left((\mu^{\star}-\bm{x}([N]))Qb-\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}\left[(\mu^{\star}-\bm{X}_{t}([N]))Qb\mid\Sigma_{t}=\sigma\right]\right)\mathbb{I}[d^{\mathrm{OL}}\neq[N]]

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{4(\beta+r_{\max})}\left((\mu^{\star}-\bm{x}([N]))Qb-\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}\left[(\mu^{\star}-\bm{X}_{t}([N]))Qb\mid\Sigma_{t}=\sigma\right]\right)\mathbb{I}[d^{\mathrm{OL}}=[N]]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 6\exp(8)S\tilde{V}_{\max}\exp\left(-CN\right)-\frac{\gamma}{4(\beta+r_{\max})}\biggl(r_{\max}\mathbb{I}[d^{\mathrm{OL}}\neq[N]]

\displaystyle\qquad+\left((\mu^{\star}-\bm{x}([N]))Qb-\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}\left[(\mu^{\star}-\bm{X}_{t}([N]))Qb\mid\Sigma_{t}=\sigma\right]\right)\mathbb{I}[d^{\mathrm{OL}}=[N]]\biggr),

where the first inequality follows from Lemma
G.22
and the second inequality from the definition of
\beta
in (
57
).
Note that
Q=(I-\Phi)^{-1}
, thus

\mu^{\star}-\bm{x}([N])=(\mu^{\star}-\bm{x}([N]))Q-(\mu^{\star}-\bm{x}([N]))\Phi Q.

Hence,

\displaystyle\left((\mu^{\star}-\bm{x}([N]))Qb\cdot-\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}[(\mu^{\star}-\bm{X}_{t}([N]))Qb\mid\Sigma_{t}=\sigma]\right)\cdot\mathbb{I}[d^{\mathrm{OL}}=[N]]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mu^{\star}-\bm{x}([N]))Qb\cdot\mathbb{I}[d^{\mathrm{OL}}=[N]]-(\mu^{\star}-\bm{x}([N]))\Phi Qb\cdot\mathbb{I}[d^{\mathrm{OL}}=[N]]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mu^{\star}-\bm{x}([N]))b\cdot\mathbb{I}[d^{\mathrm{OL}}=[N]],

where the first equality follows from Lemma
G.33
.
Therefore, we have the desired bound

\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}[V(\Sigma_{t+1})\mid\Sigma_{t}=\sigma]-V(\sigma)\leq-\frac{\gamma}{4(\beta+r_{\max})}g(\sigma)+6\exp(8)S\tilde{V}_{\max}\exp\left(-CN\right).

Lemma G.4

Consider a
N
-armed RB problem. For each
\sigma=(\bm{x},d^{\mathrm{OL}},d^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}})
, define

r^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}(\sigma)=\mathbb{E}_{A\sim\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}(\cdot\mid\sigma)}[r(\sigma,A)].

Then

\rho^{\mathrm{rel}}-r^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}(\sigma)\leq g(\sigma),

where
g(\sigma)
is defined in (
58
).

Proof G.5

Proof.
Consider the affine function introduced in
Hong et al. 2024a
:

\displaystyle\bar{r}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}(\bm{x})=r^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}(\bm{x},[N],\emptyset)

\displaystyle=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}(r(\cdot,a)-r(\tilde{s},a))\bar{\pi}^{\star}(a\mid\cdot)\cdot\bm{x}([N])+\alpha r(\tilde{s},1)+(1-\alpha)r(\tilde{s},0)

\displaystyle=\bm{x}([N])\cdot b+\alpha r(\tilde{s},1)+(1-\alpha)r(\tilde{s},0).

Notice that
\rho^{\mathrm{rel}}=\bar{r}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}(\mu^{\star})
, hence

\displaystyle\rho^{\mathrm{rel}}-r^{\pi}(\sigma)

\displaystyle=\bar{r}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}(\mu^{\star})-r^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}(\sigma)

\displaystyle=(\bar{r}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}(\mu^{\star})-r^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}(\sigma))\mathbb{I}\big[d^{\mathrm{OL}}\neq[N]\big]+(\bar{r}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}(\mu^{\star})-r^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}(\sigma))\mathbb{I}\big[d^{\mathrm{OL}}=[N]\big]

\displaystyle=(\bar{r}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}(\mu^{\star})-r^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}(\sigma))\mathbb{I}\big[d^{\mathrm{OL}}\neq[N]\big]+(\bar{r}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}(\mu^{\star})-\bar{r}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}(\bm{x}))\mathbb{I}\big[d^{\mathrm{OL}}=[N]\big]

\displaystyle\leq r_{\max}\cdot\mathbb{I}\big[d^{\mathrm{OL}}\neq[N]\big]+(\mu^{\star}-\bm{x}([N]))b\cdot\mathbb{I}\big[d^{\mathrm{OL}}=[N]\big]\;=\;g(\sigma).

Lemma
G.13
guarantees that
\bar{\eta}
and
\gamma
are well-defined. We now apply Lemma
6.7
with source kernel
Q=\bm{P}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}
and target kernel
P=\widehat{\bm{P}}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}
, benchmark
R=\rho^{\mathrm{rel}}
, function
F=g
, one-period reward
\bar{r}=r^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}
, and

a=\frac{\gamma}{4(\beta+r_{\max})},b=6\exp(8)S\tilde{V}_{\max}\exp(-CN),c=1,d=0.

Lemmas
G.2
and
G.4
provide the required drift and dominance inequalities. Therefore,

\rho^{\mathrm{rel}}-\widehat{\rho}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}(\bm{s}_{0})\leq\frac{4(\beta+r_{\max})}{\gamma}\sup_{\sigma}|(\widehat{\bm{P}}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}-\bm{P}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}})V(\sigma)|+\frac{4(\beta+r_{\max})}{\gamma}\,6\exp(8)S\tilde{V}_{\max}\exp(-CN).

By Lemma
G.30
, the model-error term is at most
V_{\max}N\delta
, where
V_{\max}
is defined in Lemma
G.24
. Hence

\rho^{\mathrm{rel}}-\widehat{\rho}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}(\bm{s}_{0})\leq\frac{4V_{\max}(\beta+r_{\max})}{\gamma}N\delta+\frac{4(\beta+r_{\max})}{\gamma}\,6\exp(8)S\tilde{V}_{\max}\exp(-CN).

Defining
C_{6}=4V_{\max}(\beta+r_{\max})/\gamma
completes the proof.

G.2
Proof of Lemma
7.3

Lemma
7.3
bounds
\widehat{\rho}^{\mathrm{rel}}-\rho^{\widehat{\pi}_{\mathrm{TS}}}(\bm{s}_{0})
, which is the second component in the decomposition (
15
). As a result, the analysis in this part is carried out under the
empirical
model.
To streamline the discussion, we first introduce the key objects and notation for the empirical system in Appendix
G.2.1
; their well-definedness and basic properties are established in Appendix
G.2.2
.

G.2.1
Definitions

As in the proof of Lemma
6.4
, to apply our Lyapunov framework we first need to ensure that the Markov chain induced by the policy
\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}
under the empirical kernel
\widehat{P}
remains ergodic, which is showed in Theorem
4.3
.

Weighted
\ell_{2}
norms

Similarly to Definition
F.1
, we define following for the empirical system:

Definition G.6

Define
\widehat{W},\widehat{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{S\times S}
by

\widehat{W}\;\triangleq\;\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(\widehat{P}_{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}}-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}^{\star})^{k}\bigl((\widehat{P}_{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}}-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}^{\star})^{\top}\bigr)^{k},

and

\widehat{U}\;\triangleq\;\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}^{k}(\widehat{\Phi}^{\top})^{k}.

Subset Lyapunov functions

For the empirical system, we can naturally define

\displaystyle h_{\widehat{W}}(\bm{x},D)=\|\bm{x}(D)-m(D)\widehat{\mu}^{\star}\|_{\widehat{W}},\qquad h_{\widehat{U}}(\bm{x},D)=\|\bm{x}(D)-m(D)\widehat{\mu}^{\star}\|_{\widehat{U}}

(60)

Furthermore, denote
\lambda_{\widehat{W}}
and
\lambda_{\widehat{U}}
as the largest eigenvalues of
\widehat{W},\widehat{U}
.

Feasibility-ensuring pair

Under the condition of Theorem
4.3
and according to the construction of the empirical primal optimal solution, we know

\{s\in\mathcal{S}:\;y^{\star}(s,0)=y^{\star}(s,1)=0\}=\{s\in\mathcal{S}:\widehat{y}^{\star}(s,0)=\widehat{y}^{\star}(s,1)=0\}.

So in terms of the empirical model, we can choose the feasibility-ensuring pair
(\widehat{\eta},\widehat{\epsilon}_{N}^{\mathrm{fe}})
as:

\widehat{\eta}=S^{-1/2}\min\{\widehat{y}^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),\widehat{y}^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}

and

\widehat{\epsilon}_{N}^{\mathrm{fe}}=\epsilon_{N}^{\mathrm{fe}}=S^{-1/2}\frac{|\mathcal{S}^{\emptyset}|+1}{N}=S^{-1/2}\frac{1}{N}.

Lyapunov functions

In terms of the empirical model, we can define the matrix
\widehat{\Phi}
as

\widehat{\Phi}\triangleq\widehat{P}_{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}}-\mathbf{1}^{\top}\widehat{\mu}^{\star}-\bigl(c_{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}}-\alpha\mathbf{1}\bigr)^{\top}\bigl(\widehat{P}_{1}(\tilde{s})-\widehat{P}_{0}(\tilde{s})\bigr).

In the same way, for any system state
\sigma=(\bm{x},d^{\mathrm{OL}},d^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}})
, we make use of the following empirical version of the Lyapunov function introduced in
Hong et al. 2024a
:

\widehat{\tilde{V}}(\sigma)=\widehat{\tilde{V}}(\bm{x},d^{\mathrm{OL}},d^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}})=h_{\widehat{U}}(\bm{x},d^{\mathrm{OL}})+h_{\widehat{W}}(\bm{x},d^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}})+\widehat{L}(1-m(d^{\mathrm{OL}})),

where
h_{\widehat{U}},h_{\widehat{W}}
are defined in (
60
),
\widehat{L}=2\lambda_{\widehat{U}}^{1/2}+2\lambda_{\widehat{W}}^{1/2}-2\lambda_{\widehat{W}}^{1/2}\min(\alpha,1-\alpha)\leq 2\lambda_{\widehat{U}}^{1/2}+2\lambda_{\widehat{W}}^{1/2}
.
Now we can define the empirical Lyapunov function
\widehat{V}
as:

\widehat{V}(\sigma)=\widehat{V}(\bm{x},d^{\mathrm{OL}},d^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}})=\left(\widehat{\tilde{V}}(\sigma)-\frac{\bar{\eta}}{2}\right)^{+}+\frac{\widehat{\gamma}}{4(\widehat{\beta}+r_{\max})}(\widehat{\mu}^{\star}-\bm{x}([N]))\widehat{Q}\widehat{b}

(61)

where

\begin{gathered}\widehat{\beta}=\sup_{\sigma}\left|(\widehat{\mu}^{\star}-\bm{x}([N]))\widehat{Q}\widehat{b}-\mathbb{E}\left[(\widehat{\mu}^{\star}-\bm{X}_{t}([N]))\widehat{Q}\widehat{b}\mid\Sigma_{t}=\sigma\right]\right|,\widehat{Q}=(I-\widehat{\Phi})^{-1},\\
\widehat{b}=\left(\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}(r(s,a)-r(\tilde{s},a))\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}(a\mid s)\right)_{s\in\mathcal{S}},\quad\widehat{\bar{\eta}}=\widehat{\eta}-\epsilon_{N}^{\mathrm{fe}},\\
\widehat{\gamma}=\frac{\widehat{\bar{\eta}}}{\widehat{K}_{Vh}},\quad\widehat{K}_{Vh}=2\max\{\lambda_{\widehat{W}},\lambda_{\widehat{U}}\}\left(1+\frac{\widehat{L}\lambda_{\widehat{U}}^{1/2}}{\widehat{\eta}\omega}\right),\quad\omega=\min\{\alpha,1-\alpha\}.\end{gathered}

(62)

G.2.2
Well-definedness and properties

Lemma
G.7
guarantees that
\widehat{W},\widehat{U}
are well-defined and their eigenvalues are in the range of
[1,\lambda_{\widehat{W}}]
and
[1,\lambda_{\widehat{U}}]
.

Lemma G.7

Under the conditions in Lemma
7.3
, the matrices
\widehat{W},\widehat{U}
given in Definition
G.6
are well-defined. Moreover, their eigenvalues are lower bounded by
1
.

Proof G.8

Proof
We first establish the well-definedness of
\widehat{W}
and lower bound its eigenvalues, following the same argument as Lemma 4 of
Hong et al. 2024a
. The key is that by Theorem
4.3
, all eigenvalues of
\widehat{P}_{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}}-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}^{\star}
have modulus strictly smaller than
1
, and the same property also holds for
\widehat{\Phi}
. Therefore, the proof for
\widehat{U}
proceeds analogously, with
\widehat{P}_{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}}-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}^{\star}
replaced by
\widehat{U}
.

Under appropriate conditions, the following two lemmas provide upper bounds, denoted as
\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{W}}
and
\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{U}}
, for
\lambda_{\widehat{W}}
and
\lambda_{\widehat{U}}
.

Lemma G.9

Under the conditions in Lemma
7.3
,
\widehat{W}
satisfies

\lambda_{\widehat{W}}\leq\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{W}}\triangleq 4S(3+\log_{2}S)\tau,

(63)

where
\tau
is the mixing time of the single-armed MDP under the optimal single-armed policy
\bar{\pi}^{\star}
.

Proof G.10

Proof
Note that

\widehat{W}=I+\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}(\widehat{P}^{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star},t}-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}^{\star})(\widehat{P}^{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star},t}-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}^{\star})^{\top}

since
(\widehat{P}_{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}}-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}^{\star})^{t}=\widehat{P}^{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star},t}-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}^{\star}
if
t\geq 1
, and otherwise if
t=0
then
(\widehat{P}_{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}}-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}^{\star})^{t}=I
.
Therefore for any
v
such that
\left\lVert v\right\rVert_{2}=1
, we have

\displaystyle v^{\top}\widehat{W}v

\displaystyle=1+\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}v^{\top}(\widehat{P}^{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star},t}-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}^{\star})(\widehat{P}^{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star},t}-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}^{\star})^{\top}v\leq 1+\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}\left\lVert(\widehat{P}^{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star},t}-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}^{\star})^{\top}v\right\rVert_{2}^{2}.

(64)

Next we can bound

\displaystyle\left\lVert(\widehat{P}^{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star},t}-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}^{\star})^{\top}v\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=\sup_{w:\left\lVert w\right\rVert_{2}\leq 1}v^{\top}(\widehat{P}^{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star},t}-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}^{\star})w

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert v^{\top}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\lVert\widehat{P}^{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star},t}-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}^{\star}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\|w\right\|_{\infty}\leq\sqrt{S}\left\lVert\widehat{P}^{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star},t}-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}^{\star}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty},

where we used that
\left\|w\right\|_{\infty}\leq\left\lVert w\right\rVert_{2}\leq 1
and that
\left\lVert v^{\top}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}=\left\|v\right\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{S}\left\lVert v\right\rVert_{2}
. Plugging this back into (
64
) and according to Lemma
B.4
we have

\left\lVert\widehat{P}^{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star},t}-\mathbf{1}\widehat{\mu}^{\star}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq 2^{-\lfloor t/(3+\log_{2}S)\tau\rfloor}.

Hence,

v^{\top}\widehat{W}v\leq 1+\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}S2^{-2\lfloor t/(3+\log_{2}S)\tau\rfloor}\leq 4S(3+\log_{2}S)\tau.

Thus
\lambda_{\widehat{W}}\leq 4S(3+\log_{2}S)\tau
.

Lemma G.11

Under the conditions in Lemma
7.3
,

\lambda_{\widehat{U}}
satisfies

\lambda_{\widehat{U}}\leq\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{U}}\triangleq\frac{1}{\mu^{\star}_{\min}}\frac{2}{1-\left\lVert\Phi\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}}.

(65)

Proof G.12

Proof
Let
v
be an arbitrary vector satisfying
\left\lVert v\right\rVert_{2}\leq 1
. Then we have

\displaystyle v^{\top}\widehat{U}v

\displaystyle=v^{\top}\left(\sum_{t}D_{\mu^{\star}}^{-1/2}(D_{\mu^{\star}}^{1/2}\widehat{\Phi}D_{\mu^{\star}}^{-1/2})^{t}D_{\mu^{\star}}^{1/2}D_{\mu^{\star}}^{1/2}(D_{\mu^{\star}}^{-1/2}\widehat{\Phi}^{\top}D_{\mu^{\star}}^{1/2})^{t}D_{\mu^{\star}}^{-1/2}\right)v

\displaystyle=\sum_{t}(D_{\mu^{\star}}^{-1/2}v)^{\top}(D_{\mu^{\star}}^{1/2}\widehat{\Phi}D_{\mu^{\star}}^{-1/2})^{t}D_{\mu^{\star}}(D_{\mu^{\star}}^{-1/2}\widehat{\Phi}^{\top}D_{\mu^{\star}}^{1/2})^{t}(D_{\mu^{\star}}^{-1/2}v)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t}\left\lVert(D_{\mu^{\star}}^{-1/2}\widehat{\Phi}^{\top}D_{\mu^{\star}}^{1/2})^{t}(D_{\mu^{\star}}^{-1/2}v)\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\max_{s}\mu(s)\right)\sum_{t}\left\lVert(D_{\mu^{\star}}^{-1/2}\widehat{\Phi}^{\top}D_{\mu^{\star}}^{1/2})^{t}(D_{\mu^{\star}}^{-1/2}v)\right\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t}\left\lVert(D_{\mu^{\star}}^{-1/2}\widehat{\Phi}^{\top}D_{\mu^{\star}}^{1/2})\right\rVert_{2\to 2}^{2t}\left\lVert D_{\mu^{\star}}^{-1/2}v\right\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t}\left\lVert\widehat{\Phi}\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}^{2t}v^{\top}D_{\mu^{\star}}^{-1}v

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t}\left\lVert\widehat{\Phi}\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}^{2t}\frac{1}{\mu^{\star}_{\min}}\left\lVert v\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\mu^{\star}_{\min}(1-\left\lVert\widehat{\Phi}\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}})}.

Hence, according to Theorem
4.3
, given
\delta\leq\delta_{\min}
, we have the desired bound

\lambda_{\widehat{U}}\leq\frac{1}{\mu^{\star}_{\min}}\frac{1}{1-\left\lVert\widehat{\Phi}\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}}\leq\frac{1}{\mu^{\star}_{\min}}\frac{2}{1-\left\lVert\Phi\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}}.

The well-definedness of
\widehat{\bar{\eta}}=\widehat{\eta}-\epsilon_{N}^{\mathrm{fe}}
requires it to be positive, which is guaranteed by the following lemma. In fact, the lemma provides a strictly positive lower bound for
\widehat{\bar{\eta}}
under certain conditions.

Lemma G.13

When
N\geq\frac{4}{\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}}
,
we have

\bar{\eta}\geq\frac{3}{4}S^{-1/2}\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}>0.

When
N\geq\frac{4}{\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}},\delta\leq\delta_{\min}
, we have

\widehat{\bar{\eta}}\geq\frac{1}{4}S^{-1/2}\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}>0.

Proof G.14

Proof
By definition, when
N\geq\frac{4}{\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}}
,

\displaystyle\bar{\eta}=\eta-\epsilon_{N}^{\mathrm{fe}}

\displaystyle=S^{-1/2}\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}-S^{-1/2}\frac{1}{N}

\displaystyle\geq S^{-1/2}\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}-\frac{1}{4}S^{-1/2}\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{4}S^{-1/2}\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}>0.

When
N\geq\frac{4}{\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}},\delta\leq\delta_{\min}
, Lemma
8.4
ensures that

|\widehat{y}^{\star}(\tilde{s},0)-y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0)|\leq\frac{1}{2}y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0)

and

|\widehat{y}^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)-y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)|\leq\frac{1}{2}y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1).

Hence,

\displaystyle\widehat{\bar{\eta}}=\widehat{\eta}-\epsilon_{N}^{\mathrm{fe}}

\displaystyle=S^{-1/2}\min\{\widehat{y}^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),\widehat{y}^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}-S^{-1/2}\frac{1}{N}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}S^{-1/2}\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}-\frac{1}{4}S^{-1/2}\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}S^{-1/2}\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}>0.

The following lemma offers a strictly positive upper and lower bound for
\widehat{\gamma}
, which guarantees the well-definedness of
\widehat{\gamma}
.

Lemma G.15

We have
\widehat{\gamma}\leq\widehat{\gamma}^{\mathrm{up}}
when
\delta\leq\delta_{\min}
, and
0<\widehat{\gamma}^{\mathrm{low}}\leq\widehat{\gamma}\leq\widehat{\gamma}^{\mathrm{up}}
when
\delta\leq\delta_{\min}
and
N\geq\frac{4}{\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}}
, where

\displaystyle\widehat{\gamma}^{\mathrm{low}}

\displaystyle\triangleq\frac{S^{-1/2}\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}}{4\widehat{K}_{Vh}^{\mathrm{up}}},\qquad\widehat{\gamma}^{\mathrm{up}}\triangleq\frac{3}{4}S^{-1/2}\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\},

(66)

\displaystyle\widehat{K}_{Vh}^{\mathrm{up}}

\displaystyle\triangleq 2\max\{\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{W}},\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{U}}\}\left(1+\frac{4(\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{W}}+\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{U}})\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{U}}}{S^{-1/2}\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}\omega}\right),

and
\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{W}},\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{U}}
are defined in (
63
) and (
65
).

Proof G.16

Proof
According to Theorem
4.3
, when
\delta\leq\delta_{\min}
, we have

\frac{3}{2}S^{-1/2}\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}\geq\widehat{\eta}=S^{-1/2}\min\{\widehat{y}^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),\widehat{y}^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\geq\frac{1}{2}S^{-1/2}\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}

It is easy to check that when
N\geq\frac{4}{\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\widehat{\bar{\eta}}=\widehat{\eta}-\epsilon_{N}^{\mathrm{fe}}

\displaystyle=S^{-1/2}\min\{\widehat{y}^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),\widehat{y}^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}-S^{-1/2}\frac{1}{N}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}S^{-1/2}\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}-S^{-1/2}\frac{1}{N}\geq\frac{1}{4}S^{-1/2}\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}.

Also, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{K}_{Vh}

\displaystyle=2\max\{\lambda_{\widehat{W}},\lambda_{\widehat{U}}\}\left(1+\frac{\widehat{L}\lambda_{\widehat{U}}^{1/2}}{\widehat{\eta}\omega}\right)\leq 2\max\{\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{W}},\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{U}}\}\left(1+\frac{4(\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{W}}+\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{U}})\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{U}}}{S^{-1/2}\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}\omega}\right)\triangleq\widehat{K}_{Vh}^{\mathrm{up}},

where
\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{W}},\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{U}}
are defined in (
63
) and (
65
), which are depend on the parameters of the original system. Hence,

\widehat{\gamma}=\frac{\widehat{\bar{\eta}}}{\widehat{K}_{Vh}}\geq\frac{\frac{1}{4}S^{-1/2}\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}}{\widehat{K}_{Vh}^{\mathrm{up}}}.

It is clear that
\widehat{K}_{Vh}\geq 2
, so when
\delta\leq\delta_{\min}
, we have

\widehat{\gamma}=\frac{\widehat{\bar{\eta}}}{\widehat{K}_{Vh}}\leq\frac{3}{4}S^{-1/2}\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}.

The following lemma gives an upper bound of
\widehat{\beta}
, which guarantees
\widehat{\beta}
will not go to infinity.

Lemma G.17

Under the conditions in Lemma
7.3
,

\widehat{\beta}
satisfies

\widehat{\beta}\leq\widehat{\beta}_{\max}\triangleq\frac{8r_{\max}}{\sqrt{\mu^{\star}_{\min}}}\frac{1}{1-\left\lVert\Phi\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}}.

Proof G.18

Proof
According to Theorem
4.3
, we know
\left\lVert\widehat{\Phi}\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}<1
. Hence,

\widehat{Q}=(I-\widehat{\Phi})^{-1}=\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}^{t},

which implies that

\left\lVert\widehat{Q}\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}\leq\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\left\lVert\widehat{\Phi}\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}\leq\frac{1}{1-\left\lVert\widehat{\Phi}\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\widehat{\beta}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{x}2\left\|\widehat{\mu}^{\star}-\bm{x}([N])\right\|_{1}\left\lVert\widehat{Q}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\|\widehat{b}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot 2\frac{\left\lVert\widehat{Q}\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}}{\sqrt{\mu^{\star}_{\min}}}r_{\max}

(because
\left\|\widehat{\mu}^{\star}-\bm{x}([N])\right\|_{1}\leq 2,\left\|\widehat{b}\right\|_{\infty}\leq r_{\max}
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4r_{\max}}{\sqrt{\mu^{\star}_{\min}}}\frac{1}{1-\left\lVert\widehat{\Phi}\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}}\leq\frac{8r_{\max}}{\sqrt{\mu^{\star}_{\min}}}\frac{1}{1-\left\lVert\Phi\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}}.

where the last inequality follows from Theorem
4.3
.

In terms of the properties of the Lyapunov function we use, Lemma
G.9
and Lemma
G.11
guarantee that under a sufficiently small single-arm estimation error,

\forall\sigma,0\leq\widehat{\tilde{V}}(\sigma)\leq\lambda_{\widehat{U}}^{1/2}+\lambda_{\widehat{W}}^{1/2}+\widehat{L}\leq 3\lambda_{\widehat{U}}^{1/2}+3\lambda_{\widehat{W}}^{1/2}\leq 3\sqrt{\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{U}}}+3\sqrt{\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{W}}}\triangleq\widehat{\tilde{V}}_{\max},

(67)

where
\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{U}}
is defined in (
65
) and
\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{W}}
is defined in (
63
).
Notice that a sufficient condition to make sure
\widehat{Q}
is well-defined is
\left\lVert\widehat{\Phi}\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}<1
, which holds under the condition of Theorem
4.3
.

G.2.3
Final proof of Lemma
7.3

Proof G.19

Proof
We work with the empirical Lyapunov function
\widehat{V}
defined in (
61
) (see Appendix
F.7
).
The next two lemmas play the roles of the drift bound and the gap-dominance bound in our Lyapunov framework (Conditions
(C1)
and
(C2)
), both stated for the empirical system under the policy
\widehat{\pi}_{\mathrm{TS}}
.
Their proofs follow the same steps as those for Lemma
G.2
and Lemma
G.4
after replacing all quantities by their hatted versions, so we omit the details.

Lemma G.20

Suppose the conditions of Lemma
7.3
hold. When
N\geq\left(\frac{4\widehat{M}}{\widehat{\gamma}}\right)^{2}
, the Lyapunov function defined in (
61
) satisfies

\widehat{\mathbb{E}}^{\widehat{\pi}_{\mathrm{TS}}}[\widehat{V}(\Sigma_{t+1})\mid\Sigma_{t}=\sigma]-\widehat{V}(\sigma)\leq-\frac{\widehat{\gamma}}{4(\widehat{\beta}+r_{\max})}\widehat{g}(\sigma)+6\exp(8)S\widehat{\tilde{V}}_{\max}\exp\left(-\widehat{C}N\right),

where
\widehat{\gamma},\widehat{\beta}
are defined in (
62
),
\widehat{\tilde{V}}_{\max}
is defined in (
67
), and

\displaystyle\widehat{g}(\sigma)

\displaystyle=r_{\max}\mathbb{I}[d^{\mathrm{OL}}\neq[N]]+(\widehat{\mu}^{\star}-\bm{x}([N]))\widehat{b}\cdot\mathbb{I}[d^{\mathrm{OL}}=[N]],\qquad\widehat{C}=\frac{\min\{\widehat{\gamma}^{2}/4,1\}}{4\max\{\lambda_{\widehat{W}},\lambda_{\widehat{U}}\}S^{2}}.

(68)

\displaystyle\widehat{M}

\displaystyle=\frac{\widehat{L}}{2\widehat{K}_{Vh}\omega}\left(1+\frac{2S^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{\widehat{\eta}}\right)+5\lambda_{\widehat{U}}^{1/2}S+3\lambda_{\widehat{W}}^{1/2}S+(\lambda_{\widehat{W}}^{1/2}+\lambda_{\widehat{U}}^{1/2})\exp\left(\frac{1}{2\lambda_{\widehat{U}}^{3}S^{3}}+32\right)\frac{16\lambda_{\widehat{U}}^{3/2}S^{2}}{\widehat{\eta}}+2\lambda_{\widehat{W}}^{1/2}.

Lemma G.21

Suppose the conditions of Lemma
7.3
hold.
\forall\sigma=(\bm{x},d^{\mathrm{OL}},d^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}})
. Define

r^{\widehat{\pi}_{\mathrm{TS}}}(\sigma)=\mathbb{E}_{A\sim\widehat{\pi}_{\mathrm{TS}}(\cdot\mid\sigma)}[r(\sigma,A)].

Then we have

\widehat{\rho}^{\mathrm{rel}}-r^{\widehat{\pi}_{\mathrm{TS}}}(\sigma)\leq\widehat{g}(\sigma),

where
\widehat{g}(\sigma)
is defined in (
68
).

Lemma
G.13
guarantees that
\widehat{\bar{\eta}}
and
\widehat{\gamma}
are well-defined.
Moreover, by Lemma
G.15
and Lemma
G.26
,

N\geq\max\left\{\frac{4}{\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}},\left(\frac{4\widehat{M}^{\mathrm{up}}}{\widehat{\gamma}^{\mathrm{low}}}\right)^{2}\right\},\delta\leq\delta_{\min}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad N\geq\left(\frac{4\widehat{M}}{\widehat{\gamma}}\right)^{2},

so the condition required by Lemma
G.20
holds.

From the empirical drift bound to a true-system bound.

We apply Lemma
6.7
with source kernel
Q=\widehat{\bm{P}}^{\widehat{\pi}_{\mathrm{TS}}}
and target kernel
P=\bm{P}^{\widehat{\pi}_{\mathrm{TS}}}
, benchmark
R=\widehat{\rho}^{\mathrm{rel}}
, function
F=\widehat{g}
, one-period reward
\bar{r}=r^{\widehat{\pi}_{\mathrm{TS}}}
, and
a=\frac{\widehat{\gamma}}{4(\widehat{\beta}+r_{\max})},b=6\exp(8)S\widehat{\tilde{V}}_{\max}\exp(-\widehat{C}N),c=1,d=0.

The drift and gap-dominance conditions are Lemmas
G.20
and
G.21
; the preceding bounds ensure that their assumptions hold. Lemma
6.7
gives

\widehat{\rho}^{\mathrm{rel}}-\rho^{\widehat{\pi}_{\mathrm{TS}}}(\bm{s}_{0})\leq\frac{4(\widehat{\beta}+r_{\max})}{\widehat{\gamma}}\sup_{\sigma}|(\bm{P}^{\widehat{\pi}_{\mathrm{TS}}}-\widehat{\bm{P}}^{\widehat{\pi}_{\mathrm{TS}}})\widehat{V}(\sigma)|+\frac{4(\widehat{\beta}+r_{\max})}{\widehat{\gamma}}\,6\exp(8)S\widehat{\tilde{V}}_{\max}\exp(-\widehat{C}N).

Using Lemma
G.32
, Lemma
G.15
, and Lemma
G.17
, we obtain

\widehat{\rho}^{\mathrm{rel}}-\rho^{\widehat{\pi}_{\mathrm{TS}}}(\bm{s}_{0})\leq\frac{4\widehat{V}_{\max}(\widehat{\beta}_{\max}+r_{\max})}{\widehat{\gamma}^{\mathrm{low}}}N\delta+\frac{4(\widehat{\beta}_{\max}+r_{\max})}{\widehat{\gamma}^{\mathrm{low}}}\,6\exp(8)S\widehat{\tilde{V}}_{\max}\exp(-\widehat{C}N).

Defining the constants accordingly completes the proof.

G.3
Other Lemmas and Proofs

Lemma G.22

When
N\geq\left(\frac{4M}{\gamma}\right)^{2}
, the Lyapunov function
\tilde{V}
defined in (
54
) satisfies:

\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}\left[\left(\tilde{V}(\Sigma_{t+1})-\frac{\bar{\eta}}{2}\right)^{+}\mid\Sigma_{t}\right]-\left(\tilde{V}(\Sigma_{t})-\frac{\bar{\eta}}{2}\right)^{+}\leq-\frac{\gamma}{4}\mathbb{I}[D_{t}^{\mathrm{OL}}\neq[N]]+6\exp(8)S\tilde{V}_{\max}\exp(-CN),

where
\gamma
is defined in (
57
) and
M,C
are defined in (
59
).

Proof G.23

Proof
We can directly follow the proof of Lemma 8 in
Hong et al. 2024a
. Showing all the constants explicitly gives us the result; we omit the details here.

The following lemma gives an upper bound of
\left\|V\right\|_{\infty}
.

Lemma G.24

Under Assumption
4.2
, the Lyapunov function
V
defined in (
56
) satisfies

\left\|V\right\|_{\infty}\leq V_{\max}\triangleq\tilde{V}_{\max}+\frac{\gamma}{\sqrt{\mu^{\star}_{\min}}}\frac{1}{1-\left\lVert\Phi\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}}.

Proof G.25

Proof
We first notice that:

(\tilde{V}-\frac{\bar{\eta}}{2})^{+}\leq\left\|\tilde{V}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\tilde{V}_{\max}.

Also, we have

\displaystyle\beta

\displaystyle=\sup_{\sigma}\left|(\mu^{\star}-\bm{x}([N]))Qb-\mathbb{E}\left[(\mu^{\star}-\bm{X}_{t})Qb\mid\Sigma_{t}=\sigma\right]\right|

\displaystyle\geq\sup_{\sigma:d^{\mathrm{OL}}=[N]}\left|(\mu^{\star}-\bm{x}([N]))Qb-(\mu^{\star}-\bm{x}([N]))\Phi Qb\right|

\displaystyle=\sup_{\sigma:d^{\mathrm{OL}}=[N]}\left|(\mu^{\star}-\bm{x}([N]))(I-\Phi)Qb\right|

\displaystyle=\sup_{x}\left|(\mu^{\star}-\bm{x}([N]))b\right|\geq\left\|b-(\mu^{\star}b)\mathbf{1}\right\|_{\infty}\geq\frac{\left\|b\right\|_{\infty}}{2}.

where the first inequality follows from Lemma
G.33
, and in the last inequality we use Lemma
G.34
by noticing
b(\tilde{s})=0
and letting
c=\mu^{\star}b
.
Hence,

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma}{4(\beta+r_{\max})}(\mu^{\star}-\bm{x}([N]))Qb\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma}{2\left\|b\right\|_{\infty}+4r_{\max}}\left\|(\mu^{\star}-\bm{x}([N]))Qb\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma}{2\left\|b\right\|_{\infty}+4r_{\max}}\left\|\mu^{\star}-\bm{x}([N])\right\|_{1}\left\lVert Q\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\|b\right\|_{\infty}\leq\gamma\left\lVert Q\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty},

where the last inequality we use the fact that
\left\|\mu^{\star}-\bm{x}([N])\right\|_{1}\leq 2
. Since
\left\lVert\Phi\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}<1
, so we can write
Q
as:

Q=(I-\Phi)^{-1}=\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\Phi^{t}.

Then

\displaystyle\left\lVert Q\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq\frac{\left\lVert Q\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}}{\sqrt{\mu^{\star}_{\min}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu^{\star}_{\min}}}\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\left\lVert\Phi\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu^{\star}_{\min}}(1-\left\lVert\Phi\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}})}.

Combining the above results, we obtain

\left\|V\right\|_{\infty}\leq\tilde{V}_{\max}+\frac{\gamma}{\sqrt{\mu^{\star}_{\min}}}\frac{1}{1-\left\lVert\Phi\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}}.

Lemma G.26

When
\delta\leq\delta_{\min}
, we have the bound
\widehat{M}\leq\widehat{M}^{\mathrm{up}}
, where

\displaystyle\widehat{M}^{\mathrm{up}}\triangleq

\displaystyle\frac{2\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{W}}+2\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{U}}}{4\omega}\left(1+\frac{4}{\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}}\right)+5\sqrt{\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{U}}}S+3\sqrt{\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{W}}}S

(69)

\displaystyle+\exp(33)\left(\sqrt{\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{W}}}+\sqrt{\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{U}}}\right)\frac{32(\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{U}})^{\frac{3}{2}}S^{\frac{5}{2}}}{\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}}+2\sqrt{\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{W}}}.

Proof G.27

Proof
According to Theorem
4.3
, when
\delta\leq\delta_{\min}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{3}{2}S^{-1/2}\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}\geq\widehat{\eta}

\displaystyle=S^{-1/2}\min\{\widehat{y}^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),\widehat{y}^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\geq\frac{1}{2}S^{-1/2}\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}.

It is clear that
\widehat{K}_{Vh}\geq 2
, so

\displaystyle\widehat{M}=

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{L}}{2\widehat{K}_{Vh}\omega}\left(1+\frac{2S^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{\widehat{\eta}}\right)+5\lambda_{\widehat{U}}^{1/2}S+3\lambda_{\widehat{W}}^{1/2}S+(\lambda_{\widehat{W}}^{1/2}+\lambda_{\widehat{U}}^{1/2})\exp\left(\frac{1}{2\lambda_{\widehat{U}}^{3}S^{3}}+32\right)\frac{16\lambda_{\widehat{U}}^{3/2}S^{2}}{\widehat{\eta}}+2\lambda_{\widehat{W}}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{W}}+2\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{U}}}{4\omega}\left(1+\frac{4}{\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}}\right)+5\sqrt{\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{U}}}S+3\sqrt{\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{W}}}S

\displaystyle+\exp(33)\left(\sqrt{\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{W}}}+\sqrt{\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{U}}}\right)\frac{32(\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{U}})^{\frac{3}{2}}S^{\frac{5}{2}}}{\min\{y^{\star}(\tilde{s},0),y^{\star}(\tilde{s},1)\}}+2\sqrt{\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{W}}}\triangleq\widehat{M}^{\mathrm{up}}

where
\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{W}},\lambda^{\mathrm{ub}}_{\widehat{U}}
are defined in (
63
) and (
65
), which only depend on the parameters of the original system.

The following lemma bounds
\left\|\widehat{V}\right\|_{\infty}
under a sufficiently small single-arm estimation error.

Lemma G.28

Consider a
N
-armed RB problem with an initial state
\bm{s}_{0}
satisfying Assumptions
4.2
,
4.2
. Suppose we have the single-armed model accuracy bound:

\max_{s\in\mathcal{S},\ a\in\mathcal{A}}\left\|\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid s,a)-P(\cdot\mid s,a)\right\|_{1}\leq\delta\leq\delta_{\min}.

Then the Lyapunov function defined in (
61
) satisfies:

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{V}\right\|_{\infty}\leq

\displaystyle\widehat{V}_{\max}\triangleq\frac{3}{\sqrt{\mu^{\star}_{\min}}}\sqrt{\frac{2}{1-\left\lVert\Phi\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}}}+6\sqrt{S(3+\log_{2}S)\tau}+\frac{\widehat{\gamma}^{\mathrm{up}}}{\sqrt{\mu^{\star}_{\min}}}\frac{2}{1-\left\lVert\Phi\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}}.

Proof G.29

Proof
We first notice that:

\displaystyle(\widehat{\tilde{V}}-\frac{\bar{\eta}}{2})^{+}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\widehat{\tilde{V}}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\widehat{\tilde{V}}_{\max}\leq 3\lambda_{\widehat{U}}^{1/2}+3\lambda_{\widehat{W}}^{1/2}\leq\frac{3}{\sqrt{\mu^{\star}_{\min}}}\sqrt{\frac{2}{1-\left\lVert\Phi\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}}}+6\sqrt{S(3+\log_{2}S)\tau},

where the last inequality uses Lemma
G.9
and Lemma
G.11
.
Then following the same argument as Lemma
G.24
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{\gamma}}{4(\widehat{\beta}+r_{\max})}(\widehat{\mu}^{\star}-\bm{x}([N]))\widehat{Q}\widehat{b}\leq\widehat{\gamma}\left\lVert\widehat{Q}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\widehat{\gamma}}{\sqrt{\mu^{\star}_{\min}}\big(1-\left\lVert\widehat{\Phi}\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}\big)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\widehat{\gamma}}{\sqrt{\mu^{\star}_{\min}}}\frac{2}{1-\left\lVert\Phi\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}}\leq\frac{\widehat{\gamma}^{\mathrm{up}}}{\sqrt{\mu^{\star}_{\min}}}\frac{2}{1-\left\lVert\Phi\right\rVert_{\mu^{\star}\to\mu^{\star}}},

where the last inequality we use Lemma
G.15
. Combining the above results we get the final bound.

Lemma G.30

Consider a
N
-armed RB problem with an initial state
\bm{s}_{0}
satisfying Assumptions
4.2
,
4.2
. Suppose we have the single-armed model accuracy bound:

\max_{s\in\mathcal{S},\ a\in\mathcal{A}}\left\|\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid s,a)-P(\cdot\mid s,a)\right\|_{1}\leq\delta.

Then for all
\sigma=(\bm{x},d^{\mathrm{OL}},d^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}})
, we have

|(\widehat{\bm{P}}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}-\bm{P}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}})V(\sigma)|\leq V_{\max}N\delta.

Proof G.31

Proof
Note that for all
t,
we have

\displaystyle\bm{P}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}V(\sigma)=\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}\left[V(\Sigma_{t+1})\mid\Sigma_{t}=\sigma\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}\left[V(\bm{X}_{t+1},D_{t+1}^{\mathrm{OL}},D_{t+1}^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}})\mid\Sigma_{t}=\sigma\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}\left[\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}\left[V(\bm{X}_{t+1},D_{t+1}^{\mathrm{OL}},D_{t+1}^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}})\mid\bm{X}_{t+1},\Sigma_{t}=\sigma\right]\mid\Sigma_{t}=\sigma\right]

Introduce the shorthand

f(\bm{X}_{t+1},\sigma)=\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}\left[V(\bm{X}_{t+1},D_{t+1}^{\mathrm{OL}},D_{t+1}^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}})\mid\bm{X}_{t+1},\Sigma_{t}=\sigma\right]

It is clear that
0\leq f(\bm{X}_{t+1},\sigma)\leq V_{\max},\forall t
. Then:

\displaystyle\bm{P}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}V(\sigma)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}\left[f(\bm{X}_{t+1},\sigma)\mid\Sigma_{t}=\sigma\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}\left[\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}[f(\bm{X}_{t+1},\sigma)\mid\Sigma_{t}=\sigma,\bm{A}_{t}\sim\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}(\sigma)]\mid\Sigma_{t}=\sigma\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}\left[\sum_{\bm{x}_{t+1}}f(\bm{x}_{t+1},\sigma)\cdot\bm{P}(\bm{x}_{t+1}\mid\bm{x},\bm{A}_{t})\mid\Sigma_{t}=\sigma\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{a}_{t}}\sum_{\bm{x}_{t+1}}f(\bm{x}_{t+1},\sigma)\cdot\bm{P}(\bm{x}_{t+1}\mid\bm{x},\bm{a}_{t})\cdot\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}(\bm{a}_{t}\mid\sigma).

Following the same argument, we have:

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{P}}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}V(\sigma)=\widehat{\mathbb{E}}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}\left[V(\Sigma_{t+1})\mid\Sigma_{t}=\sigma\right]

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathbb{E}}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}\left[V(\bm{X}_{t+1},D_{t+1}^{\mathrm{OL}},D_{t+1}^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}})\mid\Sigma_{t}=\sigma\right]

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathbb{E}}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}\left[\widehat{\mathbb{E}}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}\left[V(\bm{X}_{t+1},D_{t+1}^{\mathrm{OL}},D_{t+1}^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}})\mid\bm{X}_{t+1},\Sigma_{t}=\sigma\right]\mid\Sigma_{t}=\sigma\right]

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathbb{E}}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}\left[\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}\left[V(\bm{X}_{t+1},D_{t+1}^{\mathrm{OL}},D_{t+1}^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}})\mid\bm{X}_{t+1},\Sigma_{t}=\sigma\right]\mid\Sigma_{t}=\sigma\right],

where the last equality is owing to the following key observation: given
\bm{X}_{t+1}
, the choice of the two sets
D_{t+1}^{\mathrm{OL}},D_{t+1}^{\bar{\pi}^{\star}}
has no relationship with the transition kernel
P
and
\widehat{P}
. Hence,

\widehat{\bm{P}}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}V(\sigma)=\sum_{\bm{a}_{t}}\sum_{\bm{x}_{t+1}}f(\bm{x}_{t+1},\sigma)\cdot\widehat{\bm{P}}(\bm{x}_{t+1}\mid\bm{x},\bm{a}_{t})\cdot\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}(\bm{a}_{t}\mid\sigma)

Then

\displaystyle|(\widehat{\bm{P}}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}-\bm{P}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}})V(\sigma)|

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{a}_{t}}\sum_{\bm{x}_{t+1}}f(\bm{x}_{t+1},\sigma)\left(\widehat{\bm{P}}(\bm{x}_{t+1}\mid\bm{x},\bm{a}_{t})-\bm{P}(\bm{x}_{t+1}\mid\bm{x},\bm{a}_{t})\right)\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}(\bm{a}_{t}\mid\sigma)

\displaystyle\leq V_{\max}\sum_{\bm{a}_{t}}\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}(\bm{a}_{t}\mid\sigma)\sum_{\bm{x}_{t+1}}\left|\widehat{\bm{P}}(\bm{x}_{t+1}\mid\bm{x},\bm{a}_{t})-\bm{P}(\bm{x}_{t+1}\mid\bm{x},\bm{a}_{t})\right|

\displaystyle\leq V_{\max}\sum_{\bm{a}_{t}}\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}(\bm{a}_{t}\mid\sigma)\left\|\widehat{\bm{P}}(\cdot\mid\bm{x},\bm{a}_{t})-\bm{P}(\cdot\mid\bm{x},\bm{a}_{t})\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq V_{\max}N\delta\sum_{\bm{a}_{t}}\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}(\bm{a}_{t}\mid\sigma)=V_{\max}N\delta,

where in the last inequality we use Lemma
7.1
.

Applying the same argument to the empirical system, we obtain the following lemma:

Lemma G.32

Consider a
N
-armed RB problem with an initial state
\bm{s}_{0}
satisfying Assumptions
4.2
and
4.2
. Suppose we have the single-armed model accuracy bound:

\max_{s\in\mathcal{S},\ a\in\mathcal{A}}\left\|\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid s,a)-P(\cdot\mid s,a)\right\|_{1}\leq\delta\leq\delta_{\min},

where
\delta_{\min}
is defined in (
14
). Then for all
\sigma=(\bm{x},d^{\mathrm{OL}},d^{\widehat{\bar{\pi}}^{\star}})
, we have

|(\bm{P}^{\widehat{\pi}_{\mathrm{TS}}}-\widehat{\bm{P}}^{\widehat{\pi}_{\mathrm{TS}}})\widehat{V}(\sigma)|\leq\widehat{V}_{\max}N\delta.

The following lemma shows that if all arms follow the
Optimal Local Control
subroutine, the expected scaled state-count vector
\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}[\bm{X}_{t}([N])]
has a linear dynamics.

Lemma G.33

(
Hong et al. 2024a
)

Under Assumption
4.2
, suppose (
52
) holds for the
N
-armed system at time step
t
.
We have

\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\mathrm{TS}}}\left[\,\bm{X}_{t+1}\bigl([N]\bigr)-\mu^{\star}\mid\bm{X}_{t},\ \text{all arms follow Optimal Local Control\ }\right]=\bigl(\bm{X}_{t}\bigl([N]\bigr)-\mu^{\star}\bigr)\Phi.

(70)

where
\Phi
is the
S\times S
matrix defined in (
6
).

Lemma G.34

Let
b\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Suppose there exists an index
i_{0}\in\{1,\dots,n\}
such that
b_{i_{0}}=0
.
Then for every scalar
c\in\mathbb{R}
, it holds that

\|\,b-c\mathbf{1}\,\|_{\infty}\;\geq\;\frac{1}{2}\|b\|_{\infty},

where
\mathbf{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
denotes the all-ones vector.

Proof G.35

Proof
Let
M\triangleq\|b\|_{\infty}=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}|b_{i}|
. Choose an index
i_{\max}
such that

|b_{i_{\max}}|=M
. By assumption, pick
i_{0}
with
b_{i_{0}}=0
. For any
c\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\|b-c\mathbf{1}\|_{\infty}=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}|b_{i}-c|\;\geq\;\max\bigl(|b_{i_{\max}}-c|,\;|b_{i_{0}}-c|\bigr)=\max\bigl(|b_{i_{\max}}-c|,\;|c|\bigr).

Now consider two cases. If
|c|\geq M/2
, then
\|b-c\mathbf{1}\|_{\infty}\geq|c|\geq M/2
. If
|c|<M/2
, then by the reverse triangle inequality,

|b_{i_{\max}}-c|\;\geq\;\bigl||b_{i_{\max}}|-|c|\bigr|=M-|c|>M-\frac{M}{2}=\frac{M}{2}.

Hence
\|b-c\mathbf{1}\|_{\infty}\geq|b_{i_{\max}}-c|\geq M/2
.

Combining the two cases gives
\|b-c\mathbf{1}\|_{\infty}\geq M/2=\|b\|_{\infty}/2
for all
c
. Besides, the constant
1/2
is tight: for example,
b=(0,M)
and
c=M/2
give

\|b-c\mathbf{1}\|_{\infty}=M/2=\|b\|_{\infty}/2
.
